\doxysubsection{詳解}
\subsubsection*{template$<$typename Derived$>$\newline
class Eigen\+::\+Matrix\+Base$<$ Derived $>$}
Base class for all dense matrices, vectors, and expressions 

This class is the base that is inherited by all matrix, vector, and related expression types. Most of the \doxylink{namespace_eigen}{Eigen} API is contained in this class, and its base classes. Other important classes for the \doxylink{namespace_eigen}{Eigen} API are \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix}{Matrix}, and \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_vectorwise_op}{Vectorwise\+Op}.

Note that some methods are defined in other modules such as the \doxylink{group___l_u___module}{LU\+\_\+\+Module} LU module for all functions related to matrix inversions.


\begin{DoxyTemplParams}{テンプレート引数}
{\em Derived} & is the derived type, e.\+g. a matrix type, or an expression, etc.\\
\hline
\end{DoxyTemplParams}
When writing a function taking \doxylink{namespace_eigen}{Eigen} objects as argument, if you want your function to take as argument any matrix, vector, or expression, just let it take a \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base}{Matrix\+Base} argument. As an example, here is a function print\+First\+Row which, given a matrix, vector, or expression {\itshape x}, prints the first row of {\itshape x}.


\begin{DoxyCode}{0}
\DoxyCodeLine{\textcolor{keyword}{template}<\textcolor{keyword}{typename}\ Derived>}
\DoxyCodeLine{\textcolor{keywordtype}{void}\ printFirstRow(\textcolor{keyword}{const}\ \mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base}{Eigen::MatrixBase<Derived>}}\&\ \mbox{\hyperlink{gnuplot__common__settings_8hh_a5fd331c99e778f04762be6d8173eb4d2_a5fd331c99e778f04762be6d8173eb4d2}{x}})}
\DoxyCodeLine{\{}
\DoxyCodeLine{\ \ cout\ <<\ \mbox{\hyperlink{gnuplot__common__settings_8hh_a5fd331c99e778f04762be6d8173eb4d2_a5fd331c99e778f04762be6d8173eb4d2}{x}}.row(0)\ <<\ endl;}
\DoxyCodeLine{\}}

\end{DoxyCode}


This class can be extended with the help of the plugin mechanism described on the page \doxylink{TopicCustomizing_Plugins}{Extending Matrix\+Base (and other classes)} by defining the preprocessor symbol {\ttfamily EIGEN\+\_\+\+MATRIXBASE\+\_\+\+PLUGIN}.

template$<$typename Derived $>$ \\
\mbox{\hyperlink{_macros_8h_a9efec3cfd22b9a33bead9c6718d128da_a9efec3cfd22b9a33bead9c6718d128da}{EIGEN\+\_\+\+DEVICE\+\_\+\+FUNC}} const \mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base}{Matrix\+Base}}$<$ Derived $>$\mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base_a1e067aaf7f63281c342c8adf797a0270_a1e067aaf7f63281c342c8adf797a0270}{\+::\+Adjoint\+Return\+Type}} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base}{Eigen\+::\+Matrix\+Base}}$<$ Derived $>$\+::adjoint (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}{}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption}) const\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}}

\begin{DoxyReturn}{戻り値}
an expression of the adjoint (i.\+e. conjugate transpose) of \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this.
\end{DoxyReturn}
Example\+: 
\begin{DoxyCodeInclude}{0}

\end{DoxyCodeInclude}
 Output\+: 
\begin{DoxyVerbInclude}
\end{DoxyVerbInclude}


\begin{DoxyWarning}{警告}
If you want to replace a matrix by its own adjoint, do {\bfseries{NOT}} do this\+: 
\begin{DoxyCode}{0}
\DoxyCodeLine{\mbox{\hyperlink{_angle_axis__mimic__euler_8cpp_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a}{m}}\ =\ \mbox{\hyperlink{_angle_axis__mimic__euler_8cpp_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a}{m}}.adjoint();\ \textcolor{comment}{//\ bug!!!\ caused\ by\ aliasing\ effect}}

\end{DoxyCode}
 Instead, use the \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base_ad2a2f67a3a6e5f65f41ab47e78bbf904_ad2a2f67a3a6e5f65f41ab47e78bbf904}{adjoint\+In\+Place()} method\+: 
\begin{DoxyCode}{0}
\DoxyCodeLine{\mbox{\hyperlink{_angle_axis__mimic__euler_8cpp_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a}{m}}.adjointInPlace();}

\end{DoxyCode}
 which gives \doxylink{namespace_eigen}{Eigen} good opportunities for optimization, or alternatively you can also do\+: 
\begin{DoxyCode}{0}
\DoxyCodeLine{\mbox{\hyperlink{_angle_axis__mimic__euler_8cpp_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a}{m}}\ =\ \mbox{\hyperlink{_angle_axis__mimic__euler_8cpp_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a}{m}}.adjoint().eval();}

\end{DoxyCode}

\end{DoxyWarning}
\begin{DoxySeeAlso}{参照}
\doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base_ad2a2f67a3a6e5f65f41ab47e78bbf904_ad2a2f67a3a6e5f65f41ab47e78bbf904}{adjoint\+In\+Place()}, \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_af891b9782f712151a471b3d7cfaaa2e8_af891b9782f712151a471b3d7cfaaa2e8}{transpose()}, \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_a98af2017476fcc83444eea60afc4d15c_a98af2017476fcc83444eea60afc4d15c}{conjugate()}, class \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_transpose}{Transpose}, class \doxylink{struct_eigen_1_1internal_1_1scalar__conjugate__op}{internal\+::scalar\+\_\+conjugate\+\_\+op} 
\end{DoxySeeAlso}
\Hypertarget{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base_ad2a2f67a3a6e5f65f41ab47e78bbf904_ad2a2f67a3a6e5f65f41ab47e78bbf904}\index{Eigen::MatrixBase$<$ Derived $>$@{Eigen::MatrixBase$<$ Derived $>$}!adjointInPlace@{adjointInPlace}}
\index{adjointInPlace@{adjointInPlace}!Eigen::MatrixBase$<$ Derived $>$@{Eigen::MatrixBase$<$ Derived $>$}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{adjointInPlace()}{adjointInPlace()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base_ad2a2f67a3a6e5f65f41ab47e78bbf904_ad2a2f67a3a6e5f65f41ab47e78bbf904} 
template$<$typename Derived $>$ \\
\mbox{\hyperlink{_macros_8h_a9efec3cfd22b9a33bead9c6718d128da_a9efec3cfd22b9a33bead9c6718d128da}{EIGEN\+\_\+\+DEVICE\+\_\+\+FUNC}} void \mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base}{Eigen\+::\+Matrix\+Base}}$<$ Derived $>$\+::adjoint\+In\+Place (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}{}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}}

This is the "{}in place"{} version of \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base_a73d8782980bd75141785340d3a7a8e10_a73d8782980bd75141785340d3a7a8e10}{adjoint()}\+: it replaces {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} by its own transpose. Thus, doing 
\begin{DoxyCode}{0}
\DoxyCodeLine{\mbox{\hyperlink{_angle_axis__mimic__euler_8cpp_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a}{m}}.adjointInPlace();}

\end{DoxyCode}
 has the same effect on m as doing 
\begin{DoxyCode}{0}
\DoxyCodeLine{\mbox{\hyperlink{_angle_axis__mimic__euler_8cpp_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a}{m}}\ =\ \mbox{\hyperlink{_angle_axis__mimic__euler_8cpp_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a}{m}}.adjoint().eval();}

\end{DoxyCode}
 and is faster and also safer because in the latter line of code, forgetting the \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base_a5df64c66228ba75bbc66db2584185527_a5df64c66228ba75bbc66db2584185527}{eval()} results in a bug caused by aliasing.

Notice however that this method is only useful if you want to replace a matrix by its own adjoint. If you just need the adjoint of a matrix, use \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base_a73d8782980bd75141785340d3a7a8e10_a73d8782980bd75141785340d3a7a8e10}{adjoint()}.

\begin{DoxyNote}{覚え書き}
if the matrix is not square, then {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} must be a resizable matrix. This excludes (non-\/square) fixed-\/size matrices, block-\/expressions and maps.
\end{DoxyNote}
\begin{DoxySeeAlso}{参照}
\doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_af891b9782f712151a471b3d7cfaaa2e8_af891b9782f712151a471b3d7cfaaa2e8}{transpose()}, \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base_a73d8782980bd75141785340d3a7a8e10_a73d8782980bd75141785340d3a7a8e10}{adjoint()}, \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_a2bf1856f4ff79f0db0e6d416a2edfb0f_a2bf1856f4ff79f0db0e6d416a2edfb0f}{transpose\+In\+Place()} 
\end{DoxySeeAlso}

\textbackslash{}jacobi\+\_\+module Applies the rotation in the plane {\itshape j} to the rows {\itshape p} and {\itshape q} of {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this}, i.\+e., it computes B = J \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} B, with $ B = \left ( \begin{array}{cc} \text{*this.row}(p) \\ \text{*this.row}(q) \end{array} \right ) $.

\textbackslash{}lu\+\_\+module

Computation of matrix inverse and determinant, with invertibility check.

This is only for fixed-\/size square matrices of size up to 4x4.

Notice that it will trigger a copy of input matrix when trying to do the inverse in place.


\begin{DoxyParams}{引数}
{\em inverse} & Reference to the matrix in which to store the inverse. \\
\hline
{\em determinant} & Reference to the variable in which to store the determinant. \\
\hline
{\em invertible} & Reference to the bool variable in which to store whether the matrix is invertible. \\
\hline
{\em abs\+Determinant\+Threshold} & Optional parameter controlling the invertibility check. The matrix will be declared invertible if the absolute value of its determinant is greater than this threshold.\\
\hline
\end{DoxyParams}

\textbackslash{}geometry\+\_\+module

\begin{DoxyReturn}{戻り値}
the cross product of {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} and {\itshape other} 
\end{DoxyReturn}
Here is a very good explanation of cross-\/product\+: \href{http://xkcd.com/199/}{\texttt{ http\+://xkcd.\+com/199/}}

With complex numbers, the cross product is implemented as $ (\mathbf{a}+i\mathbf{b}) \times (\mathbf{c}+i\mathbf{d}) = (\mathbf{a} \times \mathbf{c} - \mathbf{b} \times \mathbf{d}) - i(\mathbf{a} \times \mathbf{d} - \mathbf{b} \times \mathbf{c})$

\end{DoxySeeAlso}

\textbackslash{}geometry\+\_\+module

\begin{DoxyReturn}{戻り値}
the cross product of {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} and {\itshape other} using only the x, y, and z coefficients
\end{DoxyReturn}
The size of {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} and {\itshape other} must be four. This function is especially useful when using 4D vectors instead of 3D ones to get advantage of SSE/\+Alti\+Vec vectorization.

Computes the eigenvalues of a matrix 

\begin{DoxyReturn}{戻り値}
Column vector containing the eigenvalues.
\end{DoxyReturn}
\textbackslash{}eigenvalues\+\_\+module This function computes the eigenvalues with the help of the \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_eigen_solver}{Eigen\+Solver} class (for real matrices) or the \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_complex_eigen_solver}{Complex\+Eigen\+Solver} class (for complex matrices).

The eigenvalues are repeated according to their algebraic multiplicity, so there are as many eigenvalues as rows in the matrix.

The \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_self_adjoint_view}{Self\+Adjoint\+View} class provides a better algorithm for selfadjoint matrices.

Example\+: 
\begin{DoxyCodeInclude}{0}

\end{DoxyCodeInclude}
 Output\+: 
\begin{DoxyVerbInclude}
\end{DoxyVerbInclude}

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{eulerAngles()}{eulerAngles()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{group___geometry___module_ga24e8dc29689f0075ba5b1f90c02b76dc_ga24e8dc29689f0075ba5b1f90c02b76dc} 
template$<$typename Derived $>$ \\
\mbox{\hyperlink{_macros_8h_a9efec3cfd22b9a33bead9c6718d128da_a9efec3cfd22b9a33bead9c6718d128da}{EIGEN\+\_\+\+DEVICE\+\_\+\+FUNC}} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix}{Matrix}}$<$ typename \mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base}{Matrix\+Base}}$<$ Derived $>$\mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_a052eb942d12b6404aade6fae4b075fb9_a052eb942d12b6404aade6fae4b075fb9}{\+::\+Scalar}}, 3, 1 $>$ \mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base}{Eigen\+::\+Matrix\+Base}}$<$ Derived $>$\+::euler\+Angles (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{namespace_eigen_a62e77e0933482dafde8fe197d9a2cfde_a62e77e0933482dafde8fe197d9a2cfde}{Index}}}]{a0}{, }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{namespace_eigen_a62e77e0933482dafde8fe197d9a2cfde_a62e77e0933482dafde8fe197d9a2cfde}{Index}}}]{a1}{, }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{namespace_eigen_a62e77e0933482dafde8fe197d9a2cfde_a62e77e0933482dafde8fe197d9a2cfde}{Index}}}]{a2}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption}) const\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}}

\textbackslash{}geometry\+\_\+module

\begin{DoxyReturn}{戻り値}
the Euler-\/angles of the rotation matrix {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} using the convention defined by the triplet ({\itshape a0},{\itshape a1},{\itshape a2})
\end{DoxyReturn}
Each of the three parameters {\itshape a0},{\itshape a1},{\itshape a2} represents the respective rotation axis as an integer in \{0,1,2\}. For instance, in\+: 
\begin{DoxyCode}{0}
\DoxyCodeLine{Vector3f\ ea\ =\ \mbox{\hyperlink{_tutorial___advanced_initialization___comma_temporary_8cpp_a5d2ded7a4c8830608d7b218f5f27c0a0_a5d2ded7a4c8830608d7b218f5f27c0a0}{mat}}.eulerAngles(2,\ 0,\ 2);\ }

\end{DoxyCode}
 "{}2"{} represents the z axis and "{}0"{} the x axis, etc. The returned angles are such that we have the following equality\+: 
\begin{DoxyCode}{0}
\DoxyCodeLine{\mbox{\hyperlink{_tutorial___advanced_initialization___comma_temporary_8cpp_a5d2ded7a4c8830608d7b218f5f27c0a0_a5d2ded7a4c8830608d7b218f5f27c0a0}{mat}}\ ==\ \mbox{\hyperlink{group___geometry___module_gad823b9c674644b14d950fbfe165dfdbf_gad823b9c674644b14d950fbfe165dfdbf}{AngleAxisf}}(ea[0],\ Vector3f::UnitZ())}
\DoxyCodeLine{\ \ \ \ \ *\ \mbox{\hyperlink{group___geometry___module_gad823b9c674644b14d950fbfe165dfdbf_gad823b9c674644b14d950fbfe165dfdbf}{AngleAxisf}}(ea[1],\ Vector3f::UnitX())}
\DoxyCodeLine{\ \ \ \ \ *\ \mbox{\hyperlink{group___geometry___module_gad823b9c674644b14d950fbfe165dfdbf_gad823b9c674644b14d950fbfe165dfdbf}{AngleAxisf}}(ea[2],\ Vector3f::UnitZ());\ }

\end{DoxyCode}
 This corresponds to the right-\/multiply conventions (with right hand side frames).

The returned angles are in the ranges \mbox{[}0\+:pi\mbox{]}x\mbox{[}-\/pi\+:pi\mbox{]}x\mbox{[}-\/pi\+:pi\mbox{]}.

\end{DoxySeeAlso}

The parameters {\itshape rows} and {\itshape cols} are the number of rows and of columns of the returned matrix. Must be compatible with this \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base}{Matrix\+Base} type.

This variant is meant to be used for dynamic-\/size matrix types. For fixed-\/size types, it is redundant to pass {\itshape rows} and {\itshape cols} as arguments, so \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base_a2ef43190f3aba0aef0cf1030d46d0ca7_a2ef43190f3aba0aef0cf1030d46d0ca7}{Identity()} should be used instead.

true if \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this is approximately an unitary matrix, within the precision given by {\itshape prec}. In the case where the {\itshape Scalar} type is real numbers, a unitary matrix is an orthogonal matrix, whence the name.
\end{DoxyReturn}
\begin{DoxyNote}{覚え書き}
This can be used to check whether a family of vectors forms an orthonormal basis. Indeed, {\ttfamily m.\+is\+Unitary()} returns true if and only if the columns (equivalently, the rows) of m form an orthonormal basis.
\end{DoxyNote}

\begin{DoxyReturn}{戻り値}
an expression of the matrix product of {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} and {\itshape other} without implicit evaluation.
\end{DoxyReturn}
The returned product will behave like any other expressions\+: the coefficients of the product will be computed once at a time as requested. This might be useful in some extremely rare cases when only a small and no coherent fraction of the result\textquotesingle{}s coefficients have to be computed.

\begin{DoxyWarning}{警告}
This version of the matrix product can be much much slower. So use it only if you know what you are doing and that you measured a true speed improvement.
\end{DoxyWarning}

template$<$\mbox{\hyperlink{level1__cplx__impl_8h_a4e8e1c1bb9d4e05fc671fc133ec9dc9b_a4e8e1c1bb9d4e05fc671fc133ec9dc9b}{int}} p$>$ \\
\mbox{\hyperlink{_macros_8h_a9efec3cfd22b9a33bead9c6718d128da_a9efec3cfd22b9a33bead9c6718d128da}{EIGEN\+\_\+\+DEVICE\+\_\+\+FUNC}} \mbox{\hyperlink{struct_eigen_1_1_num_traits}{Num\+Traits}}$<$ typenameinternal\+::traits$<$ Derived $>$\mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_a052eb942d12b6404aade6fae4b075fb9_a052eb942d12b6404aade6fae4b075fb9}{\+::\+Scalar}} $>$\mbox{\hyperlink{boostmultiprec_8cpp_ad5abe8020ead32dcab5e3c15d6d7c30e_ad5abe8020ead32dcab5e3c15d6d7c30e}{\+::\+Real}} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base}{Eigen\+::\+Matrix\+Base}}$<$ Derived $>$\+::lp\+Norm (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}{}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption}) const\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}}

\begin{DoxyReturn}{戻り値}
the {\bfseries{coefficient-\/wise}} $ \ell^p $ norm of {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this}, that is, returns the p-\/th root of the sum of the p-\/th powers of the absolute values of the coefficients of {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this}. If {\itshape p} is the special value {\itshape \doxylink{namespace_eigen_a7951593b031e13d90223c83d022ce99e_a7951593b031e13d90223c83d022ce99e}{Eigen\+::\+Infinity}}, this function returns the $ \ell^\infty $ norm, that is the maximum of the absolute values of the coefficients of {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this}.
\end{DoxyReturn}
In all cases, if {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} is empty, then the value 0 is returned.

\begin{DoxyNote}{覚え書き}
For matrices, this function does not compute the \href{https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operator_norm}{\texttt{ operator-\/norm}}. That is, if {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} is a matrix, then its coefficients are interpreted as a 1D vector. Nonetheless, you can easily compute the 1-\/norm and $\infty$-\/norm matrix operator norms using \doxylink{TutorialReductionsVisitorsBroadcastingReductionsNorm}{partial reductions }.
\end{DoxyNote}

template$<$typename Derived $>$ \\
template$<$typename Essential\+Part $>$ \\
\mbox{\hyperlink{_macros_8h_a9efec3cfd22b9a33bead9c6718d128da_a9efec3cfd22b9a33bead9c6718d128da}{EIGEN\+\_\+\+DEVICE\+\_\+\+FUNC}} void \mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base}{Eigen\+::\+Matrix\+Base}}$<$ Derived $>$\+::make\+Householder (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{Essential\+Part \&}]{essential}{, }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base_ab38e29219d7c456a2f225f3be092747b_ab38e29219d7c456a2f225f3be092747b}{Scalar}} \&}]{tau}{, }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base_a3242b66dcb7b06ee87b70141a47e9fbd_a3242b66dcb7b06ee87b70141a47e9fbd}{Real\+Scalar}} \&}]{beta}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption}) const}

Computes the elementary reflector H such that\+: $ H *this = [ beta 0 ... 0]^T $ where the transformation H is\+: $ H = I - tau v v^*$ and the vector v is\+: $ v^T = [1 essential^T] $

On output\+: 
\begin{DoxyParams}{引数}
{\em essential} & the essential part of the vector {\ttfamily v} \\
\hline
{\em tau} & the scaling factor of the Householder transformation \\
\hline
{\em beta} & the result of H \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} \\
\hline
\end{DoxyParams}

{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base_a7f15120bb9c88c30aa5d993bd6d138e1_a7f15120bb9c88c30aa5d993bd6d138e1} 
template$<$typename Derived $>$ \\
\mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_no_alias}{No\+Alias}}$<$ Derived, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base}{Matrix\+Base}} $>$ \mbox{\hyperlink{_macros_8h_a9efec3cfd22b9a33bead9c6718d128da_a9efec3cfd22b9a33bead9c6718d128da}{EIGEN\+\_\+\+DEVICE\+\_\+\+FUNC}} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base}{Eigen\+::\+Matrix\+Base}}$<$ Derived $>$\+::noalias (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}{}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

\begin{DoxyReturn}{戻り値}
a pseudo expression of {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} with an operator= assuming no aliasing between {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} and the source expression.
\end{DoxyReturn}
More precisely, \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base_a7f15120bb9c88c30aa5d993bd6d138e1_a7f15120bb9c88c30aa5d993bd6d138e1}{noalias()} allows to bypass the Eval\+Before\+Assign\+Bit flag. Currently, even though several expressions may alias, only product expressions have this flag. Therefore, \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base_a7f15120bb9c88c30aa5d993bd6d138e1_a7f15120bb9c88c30aa5d993bd6d138e1}{noalias()} is only useful when the source expression contains a matrix product.

Here are some examples where noalias is useful\+: 
\begin{DoxyCode}{0}
\DoxyCodeLine{\mbox{\hyperlink{_eigen_solver___eigen_solver___matrix_type_8cpp_a925331f9b6e4844293d36a8df2256d38_a925331f9b6e4844293d36a8df2256d38}{D}}.noalias()\ \ =\ \mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_addc86e8508f14411ec98f521c520f875_addc86e8508f14411ec98f521c520f875}{A}}\ *\ \mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_af60dc8214a22d8dfced07a33ec8e902d_af60dc8214a22d8dfced07a33ec8e902d}{B}};}
\DoxyCodeLine{\mbox{\hyperlink{_eigen_solver___eigen_solver___matrix_type_8cpp_a925331f9b6e4844293d36a8df2256d38_a925331f9b6e4844293d36a8df2256d38}{D}}.noalias()\ +=\ \mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_addc86e8508f14411ec98f521c520f875_addc86e8508f14411ec98f521c520f875}{A}}.transpose()\ *\ \mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_af60dc8214a22d8dfced07a33ec8e902d_af60dc8214a22d8dfced07a33ec8e902d}{B}};}
\DoxyCodeLine{\mbox{\hyperlink{_eigen_solver___eigen_solver___matrix_type_8cpp_a925331f9b6e4844293d36a8df2256d38_a925331f9b6e4844293d36a8df2256d38}{D}}.noalias()\ -\/=\ 2\ *\ \mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_addc86e8508f14411ec98f521c520f875_addc86e8508f14411ec98f521c520f875}{A}}\ *\ \mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_af60dc8214a22d8dfced07a33ec8e902d_af60dc8214a22d8dfced07a33ec8e902d}{B}}.adjoint();}

\end{DoxyCode}


On the other hand the following example will lead to a {\bfseries{wrong}} result\+: 
\begin{DoxyCode}{0}
\DoxyCodeLine{\mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_addc86e8508f14411ec98f521c520f875_addc86e8508f14411ec98f521c520f875}{A}}.noalias()\ =\ \mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_addc86e8508f14411ec98f521c520f875_addc86e8508f14411ec98f521c520f875}{A}}\ *\ \mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_af60dc8214a22d8dfced07a33ec8e902d_af60dc8214a22d8dfced07a33ec8e902d}{B}};}

\end{DoxyCode}
 because the result matrix A is also an operand of the matrix product. Therefore, there is no alternative than evaluating A \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} B in a temporary, that is the default behavior when you write\+: 
\begin{DoxyCode}{0}
\DoxyCodeLine{\mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_addc86e8508f14411ec98f521c520f875_addc86e8508f14411ec98f521c520f875}{A}}\ =\ \mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_addc86e8508f14411ec98f521c520f875_addc86e8508f14411ec98f521c520f875}{A}}\ *\ \mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_af60dc8214a22d8dfced07a33ec8e902d_af60dc8214a22d8dfced07a33ec8e902d}{B}};}

\end{DoxyCode}

template$<$typename Derived $>$ \\
\mbox{\hyperlink{_macros_8h_a9efec3cfd22b9a33bead9c6718d128da_a9efec3cfd22b9a33bead9c6718d128da}{EIGEN\+\_\+\+DEVICE\+\_\+\+FUNC}} \mbox{\hyperlink{_macros_8h_af2b60117c00a6e75812de43bfe7db3b1_af2b60117c00a6e75812de43bfe7db3b1}{EIGEN\+\_\+\+STRONG\+\_\+\+INLINE}} \mbox{\hyperlink{struct_eigen_1_1_num_traits}{Num\+Traits}}$<$ typenameinternal\+::traits$<$ Derived $>$\mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_a052eb942d12b6404aade6fae4b075fb9_a052eb942d12b6404aade6fae4b075fb9}{\+::\+Scalar}} $>$\mbox{\hyperlink{boostmultiprec_8cpp_ad5abe8020ead32dcab5e3c15d6d7c30e_ad5abe8020ead32dcab5e3c15d6d7c30e}{\+::\+Real}} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base}{Eigen\+::\+Matrix\+Base}}$<$ Derived $>$\+::norm (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}{}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption}) const}

\begin{DoxyReturn}{戻り値}
, for vectors, the {\itshape l2} norm of {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this}, and for matrices the Frobenius norm. In both cases, it consists in the square root of the sum of the square of all the matrix entries. For vectors, this is also equals to the square root of the dot product of {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} with itself.
\end{DoxyReturn}

Computes the L2 operator norm 

\begin{DoxyReturn}{戻り値}
Operator norm of the matrix.
\end{DoxyReturn}
\textbackslash{}eigenvalues\+\_\+module This function computes the L2 operator norm of a matrix, which is also known as the spectral norm. The norm of a matrix $ A $ is defined to be \[ \|A\|_2 = \max_x \frac{\|Ax\|_2}{\|x\|_2} \] where the maximum is over all vectors and the norm on the right is the Euclidean vector norm. The norm equals the largest singular value, which is the square root of the largest eigenvalue of the positive semi-\/definite matrix $ A^*A $.

The current implementation uses the eigenvalues of $ A^*A $, as computed by \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_self_adjoint_view_a66a18e7f6619ebbd3532c93da1209871_a66a18e7f6619ebbd3532c93da1209871}{Self\+Adjoint\+View\+::eigenvalues()}, to compute the operator norm of a matrix. The \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_self_adjoint_view}{Self\+Adjoint\+View} class provides a better algorithm for selfadjoint matrices.

Example\+: 
\begin{DoxyCodeInclude}{0}

\end{DoxyCodeInclude}
 Output\+:

\end{DoxyVerbInclude}

\begin{DoxyReturn}{戻り値}
, for vectors, the squared {\itshape l2} norm of {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this}, and for matrices the squared Frobenius norm. In both cases, it consists in the sum of the square of all the matrix entries. For vectors, this is also equals to the dot product of {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} with itself.
\end{DoxyReturn}

template$<$typename Derived $>$ \\
\mbox{\hyperlink{struct_eigen_1_1_num_traits}{Num\+Traits}}$<$ typenameinternal\+::traits$<$ Derived $>$\mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_a052eb942d12b6404aade6fae4b075fb9_a052eb942d12b6404aade6fae4b075fb9}{\+::\+Scalar}} $>$\mbox{\hyperlink{boostmultiprec_8cpp_ad5abe8020ead32dcab5e3c15d6d7c30e_ad5abe8020ead32dcab5e3c15d6d7c30e}{\+::\+Real}} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base}{Eigen\+::\+Matrix\+Base}}$<$ Derived $>$\+::stable\+Norm (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}{}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption}) const\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}}

\begin{DoxyReturn}{戻り値}
the {\itshape l2} norm of {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} avoiding underflow and overflow. This version use a blockwise two passes algorithm\+: 1 -\/ find the absolute largest coefficient {\ttfamily s} 2 -\/ compute $ s \Vert \frac{*this}{s} \Vert $ in a standard way
\end{DoxyReturn}
For architecture/scalar types supporting vectorization, this version is faster than \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base_a4a3ac99e4cd767a110c84421dda22ab1_a4a3ac99e4cd767a110c84421dda22ab1}{blue\+Norm()}. Otherwise the \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base_a4a3ac99e4cd767a110c84421dda22ab1_a4a3ac99e4cd767a110c84421dda22ab1}{blue\+Norm()} is much faster.

Normalizes the vector while avoid underflow and overflow

\textbackslash{}only\+\_\+for\+\_\+vectors

This method is analogue to the \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base_a82aab8b1e4a00beea9d8be19eb2f9f6d_a82aab8b1e4a00beea9d8be19eb2f9f6d}{normalize()} method, but it reduces the risk of underflow and overflow when computing the norm.

\begin{DoxyWarning}{警告}
If the input vector is too small (i.\+e., this-\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}\doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base_ace03acc2885d22588472ae898a689093_ace03acc2885d22588472ae898a689093}{norm()}==0), then {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} is left unchanged.
\end{DoxyWarning}

\textbackslash{}only\+\_\+for\+\_\+vectors

This method is analogue to the \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base_ab0b6f60e35452acd779e340856fde121_ab0b6f60e35452acd779e340856fde121}{normalized()} method, but it reduces the risk of underflow and overflow when computing the norm.

\textbackslash{}geometry\+\_\+module

\begin{DoxyReturn}{戻り値}
a unit vector which is orthogonal to {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} 
\end{DoxyReturn}
The size of {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} must be at least 2. If the size is exactly 2, then the returned vector is a counter clock wise rotation of {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this}, i.\+e., (-\/y,x).\doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base_ab0b6f60e35452acd779e340856fde121_ab0b6f60e35452acd779e340856fde121}{normalized()}.
